# JEV architecture: how to store, query and generate questions so any studio question is answerable fast (ideate, full mode)

904 options after merge: 824 positive, 80 refusals (a persona said no; kept as signal). Grounded in spec/memo: 254 quote / 573 inferred / 77 speculative. Priority = impact x confidence x query_speed, median of 3 independent scorings; fit (5 = reuses the existing pipeline) and cost (1 = weeks .. 5 = multi-quarter) shown. Verdict = contest judges on the top 25.

Positive options by part: ingestion 126, store 126, query 101, question-generation 92, ui 91, evaluation 88, query-path 53, ingestion-extraction 43, collection-protocol 19, frame-pipeline 19, session-stitching 18, feedback-loop-from-query-log 14, schema-evolution 10, export-integration 9, cost-governance 8, tenancy-access-control 6, chatbot-ui 1

## Audit: mode-collapse clusters
- Every number carries n, denominator and coverage; abstain below a threshold (about 49; e.g. askdata-1, askdata-2, stat-1, gur-3)
- Golden set or hand-labelled gate on every prompt, model or planner change (about 43; e.g. eval-1, planner-34, retr-23, stat-9)
- Studio-defined trackers (keyword + semantic + examples) backfilled over all history overnight (about 41; e.g. convo-2, convo-3, analyst-22, designer-19)
- Materialised step funnel / per-player step state machine as the fast path (about 41; e.g. latency-1, latency-15, obs-6, owner-31)
- Warehouse share or Parquet export keyed by the studio player id, with a stable versioned schema (about 37; e.g. convo-24, store-28, sec-13, datateam-2)
- Extraction provenance (model, prompt hash, schema version) on every row and answer (about 36; e.g. extract-10, store-18, kg-15, obs-25)
- Push digest or alert so the question is never typed (about 36; e.g. askdata-22, convo-29, analyst-8, platform-28)
- Silent-player handling (screen-only mode, speaking-player denominators, silence as signal) (about 33; e.g. extract-8, owner-29, askdata-18, analyst-26)
- Common data model with a per-game vocabulary map onto shared concepts (about 27; e.g. store-15, clinical-4, clinical-24, publisher-1)
- Pre/post build comparison as a first-class answer (about 26; e.g. designer-4, owner-24, platform-19, telem-16)
- Automatic churner-vs-retainer contrast (BubbleUp) with no question typed (about 24; e.g. obs-3, designer-14, owner-16, clinical-19)
- Genre-shared question bank / template library instantiated per game (about 21; e.g. qgen-18, platform-4, designer-18, convo-4)
- Unanswered question compiled into a new field and backfilled; failed-query log promotion (about 20; e.g. extract-11, planner-12, obs-32, analyst-23)
- Answer on a random sample with a CI in seconds, then refine to full (about 16; e.g. latency-5, obs-4, stat-4, tar-4)
- Variable-length episode or moment segmentation as a unit of storage (about 14; e.g. extract-14, convo-6, designer-25, owner-30)

## Audit: contested themes
- Query-time map-reduce of an LLM over raw transcripts: The skeptic prices it at about $1.30-13 and 25 s per 500-player question and argues it beats any schema for new questions. Owner, askdata and publisher refuse it for cohort numbers: it is not deterministic on re-run, too slow, and cannot be re-run to the same benchmark value. Accepted middle ground: single-session scope (planner-29), a labelled sample of 25 or fewer sessions (convo-20), or a standing nightly map (longctx-32).
- Entity-relation graph as store or query surface: The graph person makes the temporal property graph the primary store. The store designer and the analyst refuse a graph DB and path queries; even kg refuses free text-to-Cypher and time-less edges. Consensus: if a graph is used at all, it stays hidden behind tables and templated patterns.
- Cross-studio benchmarks and shared caches or banks: Platform and publisher buyers need comparable cross-title numbers and genre plan caches. Security and the data team refuse any path where another tenant can infer data. Ops refuses churn benchmarks across recruited panels, because incentive and task mode differ, and allows only PXI items and step durations. Middle ground: opt-in, k of at least 5 studios or 20 games, genre-normalised, federated execution (clinical-21), and question texts shared without answers (owner-13).
- Retrieval-then-LLM for counts and find-players-who: The analyst wants a quick count from keyword plus embedding match. The TAR practitioner, the retrieval engineer himself, and observability refuse any count or player list from top-k retrieval without a recall estimate.
- Push alerts on anomalies nobody asked about: Push is the most direct answer to unknown questions (D-critique). Asker-side refusals: alerts on unvalidated fields lose trust; analysts ignore anything not bound to a pinned metric; the data team routes all alerting through its own monitors; insights need a human accept step; clips must not go to Slack.
- Semantic cache reuse of prior answers: Latency wants answers reused by question similarity. The refusals require the cache key to include the protocol id and recall, partitioned per studio, and never shared across studios.
- Where questions come from: ontology vs hand-written vs studio runtime: The memo’s ontology generation is refused as the primary origin by conversation analytics (curated tracker templates instead) and by the skeptic. Onboarding interviews and per-game overrides are refused by the platform owner, because they do not scale to 3,000 titles. Studio runtime rewrites of core metrics are refused by the publisher, because they break comparability.
- Video-first vs transcript-first backbone; third-party video APIs: The video engineer refuses speech as the backbone. The skeptic refuses clip embeddings as primary. Ops refuses dropping think-aloud for vision. Security and privacy allow third-party video APIs only after masking, and never for pre-release frames.
- Topic clustering over all player speech for unknown unknowns: Clustering is the main auto-discovery generator. Privacy counsel allows it only over rows already tagged with a game entity, so off-game speech is never mined.
- Numbers-first dashboards and chatbot as primary surface: UX-repository requires every tile to have clips behind it. The data team, the publisher and the analyst refuse chat as the primary surface (warehouse, report drill-down, slide-ready tables instead). This challenges the spec assumption that the chatbot is the product.
- Genre-shared classifiers or banks on a new game without per-game validation: Zero-config transfer vs "recall on game A is not evidence for game B": the refusal requires a 200-session per-game validation sample.
- Telemetry inside JEV: The data team will not reverse-ETL first-party KPIs into a vendor. The telemetry scientist refuses model-estimated durations wherever telemetry has step events.

## Audit: expert-missing options
- ingestion \| ASR contextual biasing from the per-game glossary. Step, mechanic and NPC names from the extract-20 glossary are passed as ASR boost phrases, and word error rate is tracked on a 200-term game-noun list per game. Example: "Artificer's Bench" stops being transcribed as "art of fiscal bench", so the step-3 quit tracker finds 41 quitters who named it, not 12 \| ingest S, query none \| better than fixed table: the transcript the questions are read from is correct on the nouns studios ask about.
- ingestion \| Speech-to-action lag alignment. Per player, estimate the lead/lag between a think-aloud utterance and the screen event it refers to (cross-correlate utterance onsets with screen-state changes, typical -3 to +5 s), and attach each utterance to the event in that window rather than to the same timestamp. Example: "where is it" at 04:12 links to the inventory-open at 04:09 on step 3, not to the craft prompt at 04:13 \| ingest S, query none \| better than fixed table: "what did players say when X happened" counts the right utterances.
- ingestion/store \| Spatial trajectory from minimap/HUD OCR or a position telemetry hook, stored as player_path(session_id, t_ms, x, y, zone) and rolled up into a zone x minute density grid. Example: "where do players wander before quitting after the tutorial" becomes a heatmap of the hub with 37 churners circling the locked gate \| ingest M, query S \| better than fixed table: answers "where got lost" in open spaces, where a step list does not exist.
- store \| Unit-of-play table for non-linear play: play_unit(session_id, unit_type in {level, match, run, quest, boss_attempt}, unit_id, attempt_n, start_ms, end_ms, outcome, deaths, currency_delta), detected from SFX, OCR and the event spotter, with a cube by unit_type x unit_id x build. Example: "which run do roguelike players quit after" is a row lookup, as step-3 churn is today \| ingest M, query S \| better than fixed table: gives hours 2+ the same instant path the tutorial funnel has.
- collection-protocol \| Randomised build-arm assignment at recruitment: within one cohort, players are assigned build A or B at random, and the arm is stored on the session. "Did the step-3 fix work" is then a randomised two-arm comparison, not a pre/post across cohorts recruited in different weeks \| ingest S, query S \| better than fixed table: removes the cohort confound that clinical-23 negative controls can only flag.
- collection-protocol/evaluation \| Participant data-quality screen. Each session is flagged for AFK over 5 min, a second-screen pattern (speech about unrelated content plus idle input), a voice that is not the registered tester, a repeat tester across studies, or a scripted speed-through. Flagged sessions leave the denominators by default and are shown as "excluded (n=14)". Example: 9 of 41 "tutorial quitters" were AFK recordings \| ingest S, query none \| better than fixed table: churn rates are not inflated by bad sessions.
- question-generation/collection-protocol \| Saturation curve for open "why" questions: after each batch of 25 sessions, plot new reason clusters per batch. JEV tells the studio when a "why" answer has saturated (no new cluster in 2 batches) and when more recruiting is needed. Example: "why players quit at step 3" saturates at 150 sessions with 6 reasons \| ingest S, query S \| better than fixed table: says when a qualitative answer is complete, not only how precise a rate is.
- cost-governance \| Distil the frontier extractor into a small per-genre model trained on its own accepted outputs plus the golden set. The distilled model runs every backfill and model-upgrade re-extraction over full history, and the frontier model runs only on low-confidence rows. Example: a new "opened the map during step 3" field backfills over 4,000 sessions overnight at about one-twentieth of the cost \| ingest M, query none \| better than fixed table: makes "grow the question set from real use" affordable over all history.
- ingestion \| Multi-POV match sync. When several recorded players share a match or party, their videos are aligned on a shared match clock (scoreboard OCR, kill-feed and audio fingerprint), a match_id is stored, and teammate behaviour becomes a join. Example: "do new players quit after a first match where a teammate left" \| ingest M, query S \| better than fixed table: answers social and multiplayer questions, which the list currently answers only by deleting the data.
- ingestion \| Prosodic arousal track from the mic channel (pitch range, energy and speech-rate change at 1 Hz, built on the ops-9 two-channel capture) as an affect signal independent of words. Example: "players got frustrated at step 3" counts tense-but-polite and non-native speakers whose words read as neutral \| ingest S, query S \| better than fixed table: affect answers no longer depend on what players chose to say.

## Run stats
- perspectives: 30 (18 written, 12 added by the critique stage: observability, call-centre analytics, UXR repository vendor, clinical abstraction, e-discovery, video search, studio data team, studio security, publisher, platform owner, playtest ops, GUR lead)
- axes: 22 solution-space axes, 312 values (critique added 10 axes)
- raw options: 998 from 30 perspectives; 868 after in-bucket merge; 835 after cross-bucket dedupe; +69 from the audit fill (non-tutorial play structure and expert-missing items) = 904
- framing check: the audit found 86% of options used a tutorial example; the fill required non-tutorial examples
- scoring: 3 runs per option, medians by script; contest on top 25 with 2 judges
